## User
Implement Cron\Scheduler. Register add_action for both hooks unconditionally, never inside
is_admin() — a cron run is a front-end request with DOING_CRON, so a handler registered only
for admin never fires. ensureScheduled on init heals a lost event.

otherwise the reviewer activates the plugin, sees an empty table, and concludes it is broken,
because the first run is a day away.

21	/**
22	 * Orchestrates fetch → reconcile → store, and refuses to do it too often.
23	 *
24	 * The quota is 5,000 requests a month and one daily sync needs about 30 of them. That
25	 * margin disappears the moment something calls this in a loop, and two things in this
26	 * stack will: the `cron` container runs `wp cron event run --due-now` every
27	 * `CRON_INTERVAL` seconds, so a due event that never records having run is due again a
28	 * minute later; and "Update now" on the settings page is a button a person can click
29	 * twice. Both are ordinary, and both are stopped here rather than by asking people to be
30	 * careful.
31	 *
32	 * **The freshness window** is the real bound and the one that matters: nothing is fetched
33	 * while the newest stored rate is younger than 23 hours, unless the caller forces it. It is
34	 * checked against `fetched_at` in the table rather than against an option, so it is the
35	 * data itself that decides — an option that drifted from the rows it describes would let a
36	 * sync run against rates it thinks are missing, or skip one against rates that are gone.
37	 * 23 and not 24, so a job that runs at the same time each day is never refused by a few
38	 * seconds of jitter and pushed to the next day.
39	 *
40	 * **The lock** covers the seconds the freshness window cannot: two requests that both start
41	 * before either has stored anything. It is a 60-second transient — comfortably longer than
42	 * a sync, short enough that a fatal error in the middle costs one minute rather than
43	 * wedging the module until someone finds the option. It is released in a `finally`, so an
44	 * API failure does not leave it held.
45	 *
46	 * The lock is a check followed by a write, not a compare-and-swap, so two requests
47	 * arriving in the same millisecond can both take it. That is understood and accepted: the
48	 * cost of losing that race is exactly one extra request, because the second sync finds
49	 * fresh rates and stops. It is not a mutex and nothing here treats it as one.
50	 *
51	 * **Metadata is synced on a different clock.** Names, symbols and minor units change
52	 * essentially never, so fetching them daily would double the spend to re-store identical
53	 * rows. `update_currencies()` runs when the table is empty — the first sync, which seeds it
54	 * — and then at most weekly.
55	 *
56	 * A sync that cannot reach the API throws. It does not fall back to stale rates presented
57	 * as current, and it does not write a partial batch: `latest()` throws before returning an
58	 * empty payload, and nothing is stored until the whole response has been read.
59	 */

1	<?php
2	/**
3	 * The module's public function surface: one accessor, and nothing else.
4	 *
5	 * Not autoloaded — a function is not a class — so this file is required by the main plugin
6	 * file. Nothing here does any work at load time.
7	 *
8	 * @package Currency_Converter
9	 */
10	
11	defined( 'ABSPATH' ) || exit;
12	
13	if ( ! function_exists( 'currency_converter' ) ) {
14		/**
15		 * The shared converter for this request.
16		 *
17		 * Exists so the brief's own example works literally at any call site, with no wiring:
18		 *
19		 *     $converter = currency_converter();
20		 *     $converter->convert( 123, 'USD', 'RUB' );
21		 *
22		 * The service is still the deliverable — a real object with the brief's signature,
23		 * constructed with its storage, which is invariant 6 of the task contract. This is an
24		 * accessor for it, not a replacement: `new CurrencyConverter( $repository )` remains the
25		 * way to get one with different storage, and is what the tests use.
26		 *
27		 * **Shared, and that is the point.** The converter reads the whole rate map once and
28		 * holds it for the rest of the request; a fresh instance per call site would be a fresh
29		 * query per call site, which is precisely the cost the memoisation exists to remove. One
30		 * static, so a template converting prices in a loop and a widget converting one further
31		 * down share a single `SELECT`.
32		 *
33		 * Built on first use rather than at load: it resolves `$wpdb` through the repository, and
34		 * this file is required before WordPress has finished standing up.
35		 *
36		 * A theme or another plugin that needs different behaviour — a converter over fixture
37		 * rates on a staging site, say — replaces the instance through
38		 * `currency_converter_instance`. The filter runs once, on construction, so a filter added
39		 * after the first call does not take effect until the next request, and a filter that
40		 * returns something that is not a converter is ignored rather than allowed to turn every
41		 * call site into a fatal error.
42		 *
43		 * @return \Drozd\Currency\Service\CurrencyConverter The converter for this request.
44		 */
45		function currency_converter() {
46			static $converter = null;
47	
48			if ( $converter instanceof \Drozd\Currency\Service\CurrencyConverter ) {
49				return $converter;
50			}
51	
52			$default  = \Drozd\Currency\Service\CurrencyConverter::from_config();
53			$filtered = apply_filters( 'currency_converter_instance', $default );
54	
55			$converter = $filtered instanceof \Drozd\Currency\Service\CurrencyConverter ? $filtered : $default;
56	
57			return $converter;
58		}
59	}
60

2	/**
3	 * PSR-4 autoloader for the plugin's own classes.
4	 *
5	 * Hand-written on purpose. The plugin ships as a zip onto sites that have no `vendor/`
6	 * directory, so it must not depend on this project's Composer autoloader. It has no
7	 * runtime dependencies either, so its own classes are the only thing there is to load.
8	 *
9	 * `Drozd\Currency\Db\Schema` resolves to `src/Db/Schema.php`.
10	 *
11	 * @package Currency_Converter
12	 */
13	
14	defined( 'ABSPATH' ) || exit;
15	
16	spl_autoload_register(
17		/**
18		 * Load a class from `src/`, ignoring every namespace but our own.
19		 *
20		 * @param string $class_name Fully qualified class name.
21		 * @return void
22		 */
23		static function ( $class_name ) {
24			$prefix = 'Drozd\\Currency\\';
25			$length = strlen( $prefix );
26	
27			if ( 0 !== strncmp( $prefix, $class_name, $length ) ) {
28				return;
29			}
30	
31			$relative_class = substr( $class_name, $length );
32			$path           = __DIR__ . '/src/' . str_replace( '\\', '/', $relative_class ) . '.php';
33	
34			if ( is_readable( $path ) ) {
35				require_once $path;
36			}
37		}
38	);
39

## C7. "updated once a day" against WP-Cron being disabled

**Requirement:** R5 — a daily update.

**Constraint:** `config/application.php` sets `DISABLE_WP_CRON` to true, so `wp-cron.php` never
fires on a page load. A plain `wp_schedule_event()` would register an event that, on a site nobody
visits, runs never. And per trap 1, `wp config get DISABLE_WP_CRON` reports nothing — `Config::apply()`
defines constants at runtime, so a static parse of `wp-config.php` finds no definition and a
reviewer can conclude the opposite of the truth.

**Resolution:** The `cron` container in `docker-compose.yml` is the scheduler; `bin/cron-loop.sh`
runs `wp cron event run --due-now` every `CRON_INTERVAL` seconds. `wp_schedule_event()` is still the
right registration — it is the container that executes it. The freshness window in `RateUpdater`
makes the daily bound hold independently of scheduling, so a manual run, a second container, or a
misconfigured interval cannot multiply the API calls. Every configuration check in this document
uses `bin/wp eval`, never `wp config get`.

**Verification**

```bash
bin/wp eval 'var_dump( DISABLE_WP_CRON );'
# expected: bool(true) — and note that `bin/wp config get DISABLE_WP_CRON` returns nothing,
# which is the trap, not a contradiction

docker compose ps cron
# expected: running

bin/wp eval 'echo wp_next_scheduled( "currency_converter_update_rates" ) ? "scheduled" : "NOT SCHEDULED";'
# expected: scheduled
```

## C8. The API key against "never commit .env" and against `wp config get`

**Requirement:** R3 — authenticated requests.

**Constraint:** The key is a secret, so it cannot live in `composer.json`, in the plugin, or in the
repository at all. Storing it in `wp_options` instead puts it in every database dump and makes it a
per-environment surprise. And a key defined through `Config::define()` is invisible to
`wp config get`, so "the key is missing" and "the key is set but unreadable by that tool" look
identical.

**Resolution:** `FREECURRENCYAPI_KEY` in `.env`, defined as a constant in `config/application.php`
alongside the rest of the configuration, with the empty shape shipped in `.env.example` exactly as
the salts are. The settings page reads the constant and displays only presence and last four
characters, never the value. The module fails with a clear message when the constant is absent
rather than making an unauthenticated request.

**Verification**

```bash
grep -c '^FREECURRENCYAPI_KEY=' .env.example
# expected: 1, with an empty value

bin/wp eval 'echo defined( "FREECURRENCYAPI_KEY" ) && FREECURRENCYAPI_KEY !== "" ? "set" : "MISSING";'
# expected: set

git check-ignore -q .env && echo ignored
# expected: ignored
```

# WordPress plugin authoring

## When to use

Writing or reviewing plugin code: schema, hooks, admin UI, CLI, packaging.

## Where plugins live here

`web/app/plugins/<slug>/` — the whole project is bind-mounted, so files appear in the container
immediately, with no separate mount to configure.

`web/app/plugins/*` is gitignored by default. **Add an explicit negation for your own plugin**,
otherwise its source silently never reaches git:

```
!/web/app/plugins/<slug>/
```

### The plugin must stand on its own

Even though the project uses Composer, a plugin that ships as a zip runs on sites that have no
`vendor/`. Write a small PSR-4 `spl_autoload_register` inside the plugin rather than depending on
the project autoloader, and optionally fall back to a bundled `vendor/autoload.php` if present.

## dbDelta

`dbDelta()` is a parser, not a migration tool, and it is unforgiving. Every rule below breaks it
silently — the table is created, and then an `ALTER` is issued on every single activation.

- two spaces after `PRIMARY KEY`: `PRIMARY KEY  (id)`
- one field per line — the parser splits on newlines
- `KEY`, never `INDEX`
- every key named: `KEY enabled (enabled)`, not `KEY (enabled)`
- field and key names lower case
- `datetime NULL DEFAULT NULL`, never `'0000-00-00 00:00:00'` — that dies under `NO_ZERO_DATE`
- no `IF NOT EXISTS`
- always `$wpdb->get_charset_collate()`
- `require_once ABSPATH . 'wp-admin/includes/upgrade.php'` first — it is **not** loaded on the
  front end or in WP-CLI
- index short columns: an indexed `varchar(255)` in utf8mb4 is 1020 bytes, past the 767-byte
  limit of the old InnoDB row format

**The correctness check is that a second run returns an empty array.** Anything else means a rule
above is broken. Log the return value under `WP_DEBUG` and assert it after re-activation.

`dbDelta` cannot drop or rename columns. Schema v2 needs an explicit `ALTER` behind a version
option.

### Writing rows

Prefer one multi-row `INSERT … ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE` over `$wpdb->replace()`: replace is
DELETE+INSERT, so it burns auto-increment values and costs one round trip per row.

`$wpdb->prepare()` binds **values, not identifiers**. Table and column names — anything reaching
`ORDER BY` from `$_GET` — need an allowlist:

```php
$orderby = in_array( $requested, self::SORTABLE, true ) ? $requested : 'code';
```

Bind decimals as `%s`, not `%f`: `%f` casts through a PHP float and destroys exactly the
precision a `DECIMAL` column exists to preserve. Reading back, do not cast either — mysqlnd
returns DECIMAL as a string.

## Lifecycle hooks

| Hook | Does | Never does |
|---|---|---|
| `register_activation_hook` | create tables, seed data, schedule events | assume it runs on plugin *update* — it does not |
| `register_deactivation_hook` | unschedule events | **drop tables** — deactivation is not uninstall, and this is real data loss |
| `uninstall.php` | drop tables and options | assume the plugin is loaded — it is not |

Because activation does not fire on update, check a stored schema version on `plugins_loaded` and
re-run the installer when it lags.

## Admin

### `WP_List_Table`

A private core class (`@access private`) with a specific set of traps:

- `require_once ABSPATH . 'wp-admin/includes/class-wp-list-table.php'` **inside the
  `load-{$hook}` callback**, not at file level — at file level it breaks WP-CLI and the front end
- instantiate the table in that same callback, or `add_screen_option( 'per_page' )` is never
  registered and the user's choice is silently ignored
- `prepare_items()` must set `$this->_column_headers`
- `search_box()` only works inside `<form method="get">` with a hidden `page` field

### Settings and actions

- Settings API for options pages; `register_setting` with an explicit `sanitize_callback`
- action buttons go through `admin_post_*`, guarded by both `current_user_can()` **and**
  `check_admin_referer()` — a nonce proves intent, a capability proves permission, and neither
  substitutes for the other
- never echo a stored secret into a page; render an empty password field and treat empty as
  "unchanged"
- store secrets in their own option with `autoload = 'no'` so they stay out of `alloptions`

## Scheduled events

- register `add_action` for the hook **unconditionally**, never inside `is_admin()`: a cron run is
  a front-end request with `DOING_CRON`, so a handler registered only for admin never fires
- guard scheduling with `wp_next_scheduled()`
- self-heal on `init`: events vanish on database restores and can be deleted by hand
- make the job idempotent — a lock plus a freshness check, so a double click or an overlapping
  run does not do the work twice
- schedule a one-off run a few seconds out at activation, or the first result appears only after
  the full interval and the feature looks broken

## WP-CLI

Register inside `if ( defined( 'WP_CLI' ) && WP_CLI )`. Return non-zero with `WP_CLI::error()` on
failure — an exit code is what a script can act on. Support `--format=table|json|csv` through
`WP_CLI\Utils\format_items()` for anything list-shaped.

## Escaping and sanitizing

Sanitize on input, escape on output, every time — not once at the boundary:

| Context | Function |
|---|---|
| HTML text | `esc_html()` |
| Attribute | `esc_attr()` |
| URL | `esc_url()` |
| Textarea | `esc_textarea()` |
| Free text in | `sanitize_text_field()` |
| Key/slug in | `sanitize_key()` |

## Packaging

Ship a directory whose name matches the main file's slug, with a proper plugin header, a
`readme.txt`, `uninstall.php`, and no development files — no `tests/`, no `node_modules/`, no
`composer.json` unless a runtime dependency genuinely ships with it. Verify by installing the zip
on a **different** site through Plugins → Add New → Upload.


ARGUMENTS: scheduled events / cron

/**
 * PHPUnit bootstrap.
 *
 * The autoloader has to be required on the FIRST line that does any work.
 * Brain\Monkey redefines WordPress functions through Patchwork, and Patchwork can
 * only intercept files loaded after itself. Load anything testable before this
 * and the redefinitions silently do nothing — the tests then fail in ways that
 * point everywhere except here.
 *
 * @package Tests
 */

declare( strict_types=1 );

/*
 * Everything below runs AFTER Patchwork is in place, which is the only order that works.
 *
 * The plugin guards every file with `defined( 'ABSPATH' ) || exit;`, so ABSPATH has to
 * exist before its autoloader is required — otherwise the bootstrap exits silently and
 * PHPUnit reports no tests rather than an error. The value is never dereferenced here;
 * nothing under test reads a file from it.
 */
if ( ! defined( 'ABSPATH' ) ) {

 * constant. The value is WordPress's own.

if ( ! defined( 'ARRAY_A' ) ) {

#!/bin/sh
# Runs inside the wordpress:cli container as the scheduler for the whole stack.
# DISABLE_WP_CRON is true in wp-config.php, so this loop is the only thing that
# makes scheduled events fire. Deliberately NOT `set -e`: a failing tick (WordPress
# not installed yet, database restarting) must not kill the container.

interval="${CRON_INTERVAL:-60}"
echo "[cron] loop started, interval ${interval}s"

while true; do
	if wp core is-installed --quiet 2>/dev/null; then
		wp cron event run --due-now 2>&1 | sed 's/^/[cron] /'
	else
		echo "[cron] WordPress not installed yet, waiting"
	fi
	sleep "${interval}"
done
=== compose cron ===
65:  # site nobody visits: wp-cron.php is only triggered by page loads. This loop is

Any branch on it registers the handlers in\n\t * precisely the requests that never fire them.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_register_never_consults_is_admin(): void {\n\t\tFunctions\\expect( 'is_admin' )->never();\n\n\t\tScheduler::register();\n\n\t\t// `expect()->never()` is the assertion; PHPUnit needs one of its own to agree the\n\t\t// test did something.\n\t\t$this->assertTrue( true );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Activation puts both recurring events on the calendar, one interval out.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_activation_schedules_both_recurring_events(): void {\n\t\t$now = time();\n\n\t\tScheduler::activate();\n\n\t\t$this->assertCount( 2, $this->events );\n\n\t\t$rates = $this->event_for( Plugin::CRON_HOOK_RATES );\n\t\t$this->assertSame( 'daily', $rates['recurrence'] );\n\t\t$this->assertEqualsWithDelta( $now + DAY_IN_SECONDS, $rates['timestamp'], 5 );\n\n\t\t$currencies = $this->event_for( Plugin::CRON_HOOK_CURRENCIES );\n\t\t$this->assertSame( 'weekly', $currencies['recurrence'] );\n\t\t$this->assertEqualsWithDelta( $now + WEEK_IN_SECONDS, $currencies['timestamp'], 5 );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Activation also queues a run half a minute out, for both hooks.\n\t *\n\t * Without it the first rates land a day after activation, and everything in between\n\t * — the admin page, `wp currency convert` — reports an empty table. That reads as a\n\t * broken plugin, and it is the reading a reviewer reaches first.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_activation_queues_a_kickoff_run_seconds_out(): void {\n\t\t$now = time();\n\n\t\tScheduler::activate();\n\n\t\t$this->assertCount( 2, $this->single_events );\n\n\t\tforeach ( array( Plugin::CRON_HOOK_RATES, Plugin::CRON_HOOK_CURRENCIES ) as $hook ) {\n\t\t\t$kickoff = $this->single_event_for( $hook );\n\n\t\t\t$this->assertEqualsWithDelta( $now + Scheduler::KICKOFF_DELAY, $kickoff['timestamp'], 5 );\n\t\t\t$this->assertLessThan( 60, $kickoff['timestamp'] - $now, 'the kickoff must land inside one tick of cron-loop.sh' );\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Re-activating does not stack a second copy of a recurring event.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_activation_leaves_an_existing_event_alone(): void {\n\t\t$this->scheduled = array(\n\t\t\tPlugin::CRON_HOOK_RATES      => time() + 3600,\n\t\t\tPlugin::CRON_HOOK_CURRENCIES => time() + 3600,\n\t\t);\n\n\t\tScheduler::activate();\n\n\t\t$this->assertSame( array(), $this->events );\n\t\t// The kickoff is still queued: an event an hour out says nothing about whether the\n\t\t// table has ever been filled, and `RateUpdater` refuses the run if it has.\n\t\t$this->assertCount( 2, $this->single_events );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * A lost event comes back on the next request, due shortly rather than in a day.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_ensure_scheduled_heals_a_lost_event(): void {\n\t\t$now = time();\n\n\t\t$this->scheduled = array( Plugin::CRON_HOOK_CURRENCIES => $now + 3600 );\n\n\t\tScheduler::ensure_scheduled();\n\n\t\t$this->assertCount( 1, $this->events );\n\n\t\t$healed = $this->event_for( Plugin::CRON_HOOK_RATES );\n\t\t$this->assertSame( 'daily', $healed['recurrence'] );\n\t\t$this->assertEqualsWithDelta( $now + Scheduler::KICKOFF_DELAY, $healed['timestamp'], 5 );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * The self-heal is silent when there is nothing to heal.\n\t *\n\t * It runs on `init`, so on every single request. Rescheduling a live event there would\n\t * push the next run forward on every page load and the sync would never come due.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_ensure_scheduled_does_nothing_when_both_events_are_live(): void {\n\t\t$this->scheduled = array(\n\t\t\tPlugin::CRON_HOOK_RATES      => time() + 3600,\n\t\t\tPlugin::CRON_HOOK_CURRENCIES => time() + 3600,\n\t\t);\n\n\t\tScheduler::ensure_scheduled();\n\n\t\t$this->assertSame( array(), $this->events );\n\t\t$this->assertSame( array(), $this->single_events );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Deactivation clears both hooks, which takes any pending kickoff with them.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_deactivation_clears_both_hooks(): void {\n\t\tScheduler::deactivate();\n\n\t\t$this->assertSame(\n\t\t\tarray( Plugin::CRON_HOOK_RATES, Plugin::CRON_HOOK_CURRENCIES ),\n\t\t\t$this->cleared\n\t\t);\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * The recurring event created for a hook.\n\t *\n\t * @param string $hook Hook name.\n\t * @return array{timestamp: int, recurrence: string, hook: string} The event.\n\t */\n\tprivate function event_for( string $hook ): array {\n\t\tforeach ( $this->events as $event ) {\n\t\t\tif ( $hook === $event['hook'] ) {\n\t\t\t\treturn $event;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t$this->fail( sprintf( 'no recurring event was scheduled for %s', $hook ) );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * The single event created for a hook.\n\t *\n\t * @param string $hook Hook name.\n\t * @return array{timestamp: int, hook: string} The event.\n\t */\n\tprivate function single_event_for( string $hook ): array {\n\t\tforeach ( $this->single_events as $event ) {\n\t\t\tif ( $hook === $event['hook'] ) {\n\t\t\t\treturn $event;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t$this->fail( sprintf( 'no single event was scheduled for %s', $hook ) );\n\t}\n}\n"
}

```
# Decisions

Choices that are not obvious from the code, with the reason that made them. Each entry says what
was decided, why, what it rules out, and how to tell if it has been broken.

A decision here outranks convenience. If one of these turns out to be wrong, change the entry and
say why — do not leave the code and the record disagreeing.

---

## D1 — The API key travels in the `apikey` HTTP header, never in the query string

**Decided:** 2026-08-24, before the client was written.
**Status:** Accepted. Enforced by `Api\FreeCurrencyApiClient`.

freecurrencyapi accepts the key either as an `apikey` query parameter or as an `apikey` request
header. The module uses the header, always.

### Why

A query string is not private. It is copied, verbatim and in full, into places nobody audits:

- **Web server access logs.** Apache and nginx log the full request line by default, so
  `?apikey=fca_live_…` lands in `access.log` on every host the request passes through, and then in
  whatever ships those logs onward.
- **Proxy and CDN caches.** The query string is part of the cache key, so intermediaries retain it
  by design.
- **Query Monitor.** The HTTP panel lists outbound requests with their full URLs. Any administrator
  who opens it on a page that triggered a sync reads the live key off the screen.
- **The transcripts this project ships.** `.claude/agents/_TASK_CONTRACT.md` R11 requires exported
  AI sessions in `docs/transcripts/` as a deliverable. Those transcripts quote commands, responses
  and debugging output. A key in a URL is a key in the submitted deliverable — a repository, an
  archive, and a reviewer's inbox.

The last one is the reason this is written down rather than assumed. The other three are ordinary
operational hygiene and would justify the header on their own; the fourth means a mistake here is
not merely logged somewhere unfortunate but actively published, and cannot be walked back by
rotating a key after the fact — the transcript keeps the old one.

A header avoids all four. It is not logged by default, is not part of a cache key, is not rendered
by Query Monitor's URL column, and does not appear in the shell commands a transcript records.

### What this rules out

- No `add_query_arg( 'apikey', … )`, anywhere.
- No debugging convenience that pastes a full URL with the key into a log line, a notice, an
  exception message or a comment.
- `probe-api.sh` sends the key with `curl -H "apikey: …"` for the same reason, and prints only the
  last four characters of it.

### How to tell if it has been broken

```bash
# The key must never appear in a URL or a query-building call.
grep -rn "apikey=" web/app/plugins/currency-converter/ .claude/skills/freecurrencyapi/scripts/
# expected: nothing

# Real key material must not be in any tracked file. Placeholders (fca_live_xxx, fca_live_…)
# are documentation and are expected; a match of 16+ real characters is not.
git grep -nE 'fca_live_[A-Za-z0-9]{16,}' | grep -viE 'x{16,}'
...
11:## D1 — The API key travels in the `apikey` HTTP header, never in the query string
77:## D2 — Transport failures are an exception, not a response with status 0
108:## D3 — The observed monthly quota is recorded from response headers, not polled
152:## D4 — The converter rounds half-up through `bcmath`, never `round()` on a float
```

</details>

## Assistant
Done. `Cron\Scheduler` is in [Scheduler.php](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Cron/Scheduler.php), wired through [Plugin.php](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Plugin.php), with [SchedulerTest.php](tests/Cron/SchedulerTest.php) covering the parts that fail silently.

**What it does**

- [`register()`](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Cron/Scheduler.php#L74-L83) — three unguarded `add_action()` calls: both cron hooks and `init`. Called from `Plugin::boot()` at file scope, so the handlers exist in a cron request, which is a front-end request. A test asserts `is_admin()` is never even consulted.
- [`activate()`](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Cron/Scheduler.php#L94-L103) — recurring `daily` anchored a day out and `weekly` a week out, plus a single event 30 s out on **both** hooks. The metadata one is there for the same reason as the rates one: 33 rates with no names or symbols looks as unfinished as an empty table.
- [`ensure_scheduled()`](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Cron/Scheduler.php#L120-L123) on `init` re-creates a lost event 30 s out rather than a full interval out — an event that vanished has probably been gone a while. That is free when the data is actually current, because `RateUpdater`'s freshness window answers "fresh" without spending quota.
- [`run()`](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Cron/Scheduler.php#L196-L206) catches `\Throwable` and logs it. Letting it escape would abort the whole `--due-now` sweep and take every event queued behind it with it. Nothing is retried and nothing falls back to stale rates — the next daily run is the retry.

One correction to my first pass: `error_log()` alone was wrong here. `WP_DEBUG_LOG` is true in development, so WordPress redirects it to `web/app/debug.log` and nothing reaches the cron container's stdout. The logger now also goes through `WP_CLI::log()` when running under WP-CLI, which is what puts the line in `docker compose logs cron`.

**`bin/wp cron event list`** — immediately after activation, showing the two kickoffs alongside the recurring pair:

```
hook                                  next_run_gmt         next_run_relative     recurrence
currency_converter_update_rates       2026-08-24 18:44:01  28 seconds            Non-repeating
currency_converter_update_currencies  2026-08-24 18:44:01  28 seconds            Non-repeating
currency_converter_update_rates       2026-08-25 18:43:31  23 hours 59 minutes   1 day
currency_converter_update_currencies  2026-08-31 18:43:31  6 days 23 hours       1 week
```

**`docker compose logs cron`** — the next tick consuming them:

```
cron-1  | [cron] currency-converter: currency_converter_update_rates: Skipped: rates are fresh (last sync 2026-08-24 18:07:58 UTC)
cron-1  | [cron] Executed the cron event 'currency_converter_update_rates' in 0.007s.
cron-1  | [cron] currency-converter: currency_converter_update_currencies: Skipped: currency metadata is current (last synced 2026-08-24 18:07:58 UTC)
cron-1  | [cron] Executed the cron event 'currency_converter_update_currencies' in 0.001s.
cron-1  | [cron] Success: Executed a total of 2 cron events.
```

Both skipped as fresh against data from 18:07 today, so no API call and no quota spent — which is the freshness window doing exactly its job on a kickoff that landed on top of current data.

`make lint` and `make test` are green (170 tests). One thing you'll want to know: `FREECURRENCYAPI_KEY` is not set in `.env` on this machine, so the first sync that *isn't* skipped will log `No freecurrencyapi key is configured; refusing to send an unauthenticated request` rather than fetch. The client refuses locally, so that costs nothing, but a reviewer starting from a fresh table would see a failure line instead of rates.

1	<?php
2	/**
3	 * Exchange rate storage on `$wpdb`.
4	 *
5	 * @package Currency_Converter
6	 */
7	
8	namespace Drozd\Currency\Db;
9	
10	use Drozd\Currency\Domain\Currency;
11	use Drozd\Currency\Domain\Rate;
12	use Drozd\Currency\Domain\RateRepositoryInterface;
13	
14	defined( 'ABSPATH' ) || exit;
15	
16	/**
17	 * Reads and writes `{$wpdb->prefix}cc_rates`.
18	 *
19	 * One of the two files in the module that know what a database is. Everything above it
20	 * works with `Rate` objects and decimal strings.
21	 *
22	 * Four decisions are load-bearing here, and each one is a bug avoided rather than a
23	 * preference:
24	 *
25	 * **One statement, not one per row.** The whole batch goes out as a single multi-row
26	 * `INSERT ... ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE`. The obvious alternative, `$wpdb->replace()` in a
27	 * loop, is wrong twice over: `REPLACE` is a `DELETE` followed by an `INSERT`, so it burns
28	 * 33 auto-increment values every single day and would exhaust a signed `INT` id in a decade
29	 * of daily syncs — and it is 33 network round trips where one will do.
30	 *
31	 * **`%s` binds the rate, never `%f`.** `%f` formats through the current locale, so under a
32	 * `de_DE` locale it would bind `93,0071` and MySQL would read that as `93`. It is lossy as
33	 * well: the float it formats has fewer significant digits than `DECIMAL(24,12)` holds.
34	 *
35	 * **A decimal read back is a string and stays one.** No `(float)` appears in this file.
36	 * Casting on the way out throws away the precision the column exists to keep.
37	 *
38	 * **`ORDER BY` comes from an allowlist.** `orderby` and `order` arrive from `$_GET` on the
39	 * admin screen, and `$wpdb->prepare()` binds *values*, not identifiers — there is no
40	 * placeholder for a column name. A requested column is matched against a fixed list and the
41	 * default is used when it does not match, so nothing from the request is ever interpolated.
42	 */
43	final class WpdbRateRepository implements RateRepositoryInterface {
44	
45		/**
46		 * Columns a caller may sort by, and the only strings that can reach `ORDER BY`.
47		 */
48		const SORTABLE_COLUMNS = array( 'base_code', 'target_code', 'rate', 'fetched_at' );
49	
50		/**
51		 * Column sorted on when the request asks for one that is not on the allowlist.
52		 */
53		const DEFAULT_ORDERBY = 'target_code';
54	
55		/**
56		 * Rows per page when the caller does not say.
57		 *
58		 * Above today's 33, so the admin page shows every stored rate on one screen while the
59		 * single-base design holds, and pages properly if a paid plan ever adds bases.
60		 */
61		const DEFAULT_PER_PAGE = 50;
62	
63		/**
64		 * Ceiling on `per_page`, so a crafted `?per_page=100000` cannot ask for everything.
65		 */
66		const MAX_PER_PAGE = 500;
67	
68		/**
69		 * Rows per `INSERT` statement.
70		 *
71		 * Today's batch is 33 and goes out as exactly one statement. The chunking is for the
72		 * case the schema was designed for — `base_code` is a real column, so a paid plan turns
73		 * 33 rows into 33 × 33 — where one statement would approach `max_allowed_packet`. It is
74		 * never one statement per row.
75		 */
76		const MAX_ROWS_PER_STATEMENT = 100;
77	
78		/**
79		 * Database handle.
80		 *
81		 * @var \wpdb
82		 */
83		private $wpdb;
84	
85		/**
86		 * Prefixed table name, resolved once from the handle above.
87		 *
88		 * @var string
89		 */
90		private $table;
91	
92		/**
93		 * Memoised rate maps, keyed by base code.
94		 *

21	/**
22	 * The service the brief asks for: `$converter->convert( 123, 'USD', 'RUB' )`.
23	 *
24	 * An object with that method, constructed with its storage, and not a static helper, a
25	 * bare function or a filter — that is invariant 6 of the task contract, and the signature
26	 * is taken from the brief verbatim, float in and float out.
27	 *
28	 * Three decisions carry the class, and each one is the difference between a service and a
29	 * script that happens to multiply two numbers.
30	 *
31	 * **One query per request, not one per call.** `convert()` in a loop over a cart, a price
32	 * list or a report is the normal way this gets used, and the naive implementation is one
33	 * `SELECT` per call. The whole rate map for the stored base is read once, on the first
34	 * conversion that needs it, and held on the instance for the rest of the request — which is
35	 * why the module hands callers a shared instance through `currency_converter()` rather than
36	 * letting every call site build its own. An empty table memoises as an empty map, so a site
37	 * whose sync has never run still costs one query rather than one per call; every conversion
38	 * then throws, which is the point.
39	 *
40	 * Memoisation is per *request*, deliberately: it is an object property and not an object
41	 * cache or a transient, so nothing outlives the response and a rate written by cron is
42	 * picked up by the next request instead of going stale for a TTL. A long-running process
43	 * — the CLI, cron — that writes rates and then converts calls `flush()`.
44	 *
45	 * **The arithmetic is `bcmath`, and the rounding is half-up on decimal strings.** Rates are
46	 * `DECIMAL(24,12)` and are read as strings precisely so the twelve stored decimal places
47	 * survive; doing the multiplication in floating point would throw that away in the last step
48	 * of the one operation the module exists to perform. `round()` is the specific trap: it is
49	 * handed a float that is already off by a fraction of an ulp, so `round( 2.675, 2 )` is
50	 * `2.67` — the value it rounds is really `2.67499999999999982…`, and no amount of care at
51	 * the call site fixes that. Every product, quotient and rounding decision here is made on
52	 * exact decimal strings. The float cast happens once, at the very end, because the brief's
53	 * signature says the method returns one.
54	 *
55	 * **A missing rate throws.** It is not a rate of 1, not yesterday's rate presented as
56	 * today's, and not zero — the rule in the task contract that overrides "make it work". The
57	 * two failures are separate types on purpose: `UnknownCurrencyException` means the caller
58	 * asked for something that is not a currency this module serves (a typo, fix the code),
59	 * `RatesUnavailableException` means the currency is real and the sync has not stored a rate
60	 * for it (fix the cron job or the API key). One "conversion failed" type would send whoever
61	 * reads the log looking for the wrong thing.
62	 *
63	 * Framework-free: no WordPress function is called in this file. Storage arrives as
64	 * `RateRepositoryInterface`, so the arithmetic below is tested against an in-memory
65	 * implementation with no database and no bootstrap. `bcmath` is the one extension it needs,
66	 * and it is present in both of this project's images — checked in both, because a check that

```
## C2. "all saved exchange rates" against pagination

**Requirement:** R7 — the admin page displays "all saved exchange rates".

**Constraint:** "All" and "one screen" are the same demand only while the table is small. It is 33
rows today, 33 × 33 = 1,089 the moment a paid plan adds bases, and unbounded if historical rows are
ever kept. An unpaginated `WP_List_Table` loads every row into memory and renders it, which is the
standard way admin screens fall over on real data.

**Resolution:** "All" is read as *reachable and accounted for*, not *simultaneously on screen*.
`WP_List_Table` with a default of 50 per page — above today's 33, so the literal reading also holds
at present scale — a screen-options control for per-page, sortable columns, and a header that
prints the **total** count from `SELECT COUNT(*)`, not the count of the current page. The total is
what proves nothing is hidden: it is checkable against the database independently of paging. A CSV
export (`wp currency list --format=csv`) gives the genuinely complete dump for anyone who needs one
file.

**Verification**

```bash
bin/wp currency list --format=count
docker compose exec -T db sh -c 'mariadb -u"$MARIADB_USER" -p"$MARIADB_PASSWORD" "$MARIADB_DATABASE" -N -e "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM wp_cc_rates"'
# expected: the two numbers are equal, and both equal the "N items" in docs/screenshots/currency-rates.png
```

## C3. "any suitable PHP-framework" against WordPress being a CMS

**Requirement:** R10 — "Any suitable PHP-framework can be used".

**Constraint:** WordPress is a CMS, not a framework in the Laravel/Symfony sense. It offers no DI
container, no ORM, no router usable from a module, no first-class service layer; it supplies global
functions, hooks, `$wpdb`, and a plugin lifecycle. A reviewer expecting `App\Services\Converter`
wired by a container will not find one. The brief's "suitable" is doing the work here.

**Resolution:** WordPress is chosen deliberately, and the mismatch is absorbed rather than ignored.
The module is a plugin, PSR-4 autoloaded under `Drozd\Currency\`, with constructor injection by hand
— `new Converter( new RateRepository() )` — so the domain classes stay framework-shaped even though
the platform is not. WordPress is confined to the edges: `wp_remote_get` in `Api\Client`, `$wpdb` in
`Repository\RateRepository`, hooks in `Plugin`, `WP_List_Table` in `Admin\`. `Converter` itself
touches no WordPress function, which is why it can be unit-tested with Brain\Monkey and no database.
## C6. Money arithmetic and formatting against the extension sets of two different images

**Requirement:** R6 — money arithmetic; R4's stored precision; R7 — rates rendered in the admin.

**Constraint:** `CLAUDE.md` trap 2: `app` (`php:8.3-apache`, built from our `Dockerfile`) and
`wpcli`/`cron` (the official Alpine WP-CLI image) do not carry the same extension set, and a check
that only ran through `bin/wp` says nothing about what a browser request can do. The current split,
verified rather than assumed:

| Extension | `app` | `wpcli` / `cron` | Consequence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `bcmath` | yes (`Dockerfile`) | yes | safe in both — exact decimal arithmetic is available |
| `intl` | **no** | yes | `NumberFormatter` fatals in the browser, passes every `bin/wp` check |

`intl` is the live hazard for this module, and a currency module is exactly the code that reaches
for it: `NumberFormatter::CURRENCY` is the obvious way to render a rate in the admin table (R7),
it would work in every WP-CLI test, and it would fatal on the first admin page load. Its absence
from `app` is deliberate — nothing depends on it and it drags in libicu.

Note that `CLAUDE.md` still names `bcmath` as the CLI-only extension. That was true when it was
written and is now stale: `bcmath` was added to the `Dockerfile` and is in both. The trap is real;
its example has moved.

**Resolution:** No new extension, and no `intl`. Rates are stored as `DECIMAL(24,12)` so the
database keeps full precision
… (truncated)
```

	 * A successful call spends quota like any other, so this is for deliberate checks,

	 *
	 * @return array<string, mixed> The `quotas` object: `month` and `grace` buckets.

	public function status() {
		$decoded = $this->request( '/status' );

		if ( ! isset( $decoded['quotas'] ) || ! is_array( $decoded['quotas'] ) ) {

				'freecurrencyapi returned HTTP 200 with no quota information',
				200
			);
		}

		return $decoded['quotas'];
	}

	 * @return array{remaining:int, limit:int|null, checked_at:int}|null Last reading, or null.
	 */
	public static function stored_quota() {
		$stored = get_option( self::QUOTA_OPTION );

',256}$/', $key );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * The key the environment supplies.\n\t *\n\t * @return string The constant's value, trimmed; empty when it is unset or blank.\n\t */\n\tprivate static function from_environment() {\n\t\tif ( ! defined( self::CONSTANT ) ) {\n\t\t\treturn '';\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t$value = constant( self::CONSTANT );\n\n\t\treturn is_string( $value ) ? trim( $value ) : '';\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * The key an administrator stored.\n\t *\n\t * @return string The option's value, trimmed; empty when unset.\n\t */\n\tprivate static function from_option() {\n\t\t$stored = get_option( self::OPTION, '' );\n\n\t\treturn is_string( $stored ) ? trim( $stored ) : '';\n\t}\n}\n"

That is not the\n\t * same job as `prepare()` and neither substitutes for the other: `prepare()` stops the\n\t * value being read as SQL, `esc_like()` stops a literal `%` or `_` inside it being read\n\t * as a wildcard by `LIKE`. Without the first it is an injection; without the second a\n\t * search for `_` quietly matches everything.\n\t *\n\t * @param array<string, mixed> $args Query arguments; `base_code` and `search` are read.\n\t * @return array{0: string, 1: array<int, string>} The clause (with a leading space, or\n\t *                                                  empty) and the values to bind.\n\t */\n\tprivate function where_clause( array $args ) {\n\t\t$base   = self::base_filter( isset( $args['base_code'] ) ? $args['base_code'] : '' );\n\t\t$search = isset( $args['search'] ) && is_string( $args['search'] ) ? trim( $args['search'] ) : '';\n\n\t\t$clauses = array();\n\t\t$params  = array();\n\n\t\tif ( '' !== $base ) {\n\t\t\t$clauses[] = 'base_code = %s';\n\t\t\t$params[]  = $base;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif ( '' !== $search ) {\n\t\t\t$like = '%' . $this->wpdb->esc_like( $search ) . '%';\n\n\t\t\t// Both codes, so \"EUR\" finds the pair from either side. The currency *name* is in\n\t\t\t// the other table and is deliberately not joined in: a join here would make the\n\t\t\t// count and the rows depend on whether the metadata sync has run.\n\t\t\t$clauses[] = '(base_code LIKE %s OR target_code LIKE %s)';\n\t\t\t$params[]  = $like;\n\t\t\t$params[]  = $like;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif ( array() === $clauses ) {\n\t\t\treturn array( '', array() );\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn array( ' WHERE ' . implode( ' AND ', $clauses ), $params );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Reduce a requested sort column to one this table actually has."
}

'F', (float) $value ) !== sprintf( '%.' . $decimals . 'F', (float) $trimmed )\n\t\t\t|| 0 !== bccomp( sprintf( '%.' . Rate::SCALE . 'F', (float) $value ), (string) $value, Rate::SCALE ) ) {\n\t\t\treturn (string) $value;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn number_format_i18n( (float) $trimmed, $decimals );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * The current search term.\n\t *\n\t * @return string Sanitised term, or an empty string.\n\t */\n\tprivate static function requested_search() {\n\t\treturn self::requested( 's' );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * One value from the query string, sanitised.\n\t *\n\t * No nonce is checked and none is wanted.   "content": "<?php\n/**\n * The admin conversion widget's endpoint.\n *\n * @package Currency_Converter\n */\n\nnamespace Drozd\\Currency\\Admin;\n\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Db\\WpdbCurrencyRepository;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Domain\\Currency;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Exception\\ExceptionInterface;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Service\\CurrencyConverter;\n\ndefined( 'ABSPATH' ) || exit;\n\n/**\n * `admin-ajax.php` in front of `Service\\CurrencyConverter`.\n *\n * The widget converts through the same object every other caller uses, over the wire. The\n * arithmetic deliberately does not happen in the browser: rates are `DECIMAL(24,12)` and\n * JavaScript has one number type, an IEEE-754 double, which cannot hold them — a widget that\n * multiplied in JavaScript would disagree with `convert()` in the last decimal places and\n * would be demonstrating something other than the module.\n *\n * **Both guards, because they answer different questions.** `check_ajax_referer()` proves the\n * request came from a page this site rendered — it stops another origin from driving the\n * endpoint with the administrator's cookies. `current_user_can()` proves the person behind it\n * is allowed to be here — it stops a logged-in subscriber who read the nonce out of a page\n * they *can* see. Neither implies the other, and a nonce alone is the more common mistake.\n *\n * Only `wp_ajax_` is registered, never `wp_ajax_nopriv_`. The widget belongs to a\n * `manage_options` screen, so there is no logged-out caller to serve, and registering the\n * public variant would publish a rate endpoint the brief did not ask for — a REST endpoint\n * by another name, and a non-goal of the task contract.\n */\nfinal class ConvertAjax {\n\n\t/**\n\t * The AJAX action name.\n\t */\n\tconst ACTION = 'currency_converter_convert';\n\n\t/**\n\t * The nonce action.\n\t */\n\tconst NONCE = 'currency_converter_convert';\n\n\t/**\n\t * Longest amount string accepted.\n\t *\n\t * `bcmath` is happy with arbitrarily long input and would spend real time on a megabyte\n\t * of digits. `DECIMAL(24,12)` and any plausible amount fit inside this.\n\t */\n\tconst MAX_AMOUNT_LENGTH = 32;\n\n\t/**\n\t * Register the endpoint.\n\t *\n\t * @return void\n\t */\n\tpublic static function register() {\n\t\tadd_action( 'wp_ajax_' . self::ACTION, array( self::class, 'handle' ) );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Convert, and answer in JSON.\n\t *\n\t * @return void\n\t */\n\tpublic static function handle() {\n\t\t// Dies with -1 on failure, which is what the front end expects from admin-ajax.\n\t\tcheck_ajax_referer( self::NONCE, 'nonce' );\n\n\t\tif ( ! current_user_can( Menu::CAPABILITY ) ) {\n\t\t\twp_send_json_error(\n\t\t\t\tarray( 'message' => __( 'You do not have permission to convert currencies.', 'currency-converter' ) ),\n\t\t\t\t403\n\t\t\t);\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t$amount = self::posted( 'amount' );\n\t\t$from   = self::posted( 'from' );\n\t\t$to     = self::posted( 'to' );\n\n\t\tif ( strlen( $amount ) > self::MAX_AMOUNT_LENGTH ) {\n\t\t\twp_send_json_error(\n\t\t\t\tarray( 'message' => __( 'That amount is too long to be a number.', 'currency-converter' ) ),\n\t\t\t\t400\n\t\t\t);\n\t\t}\n\n\t\ttry {\n\t\t\t$converter = currency_converter();\n\n\t\t\t$result = $converter->convert( $amount, $from, $to );\n\t\t\t$rate   = $converter->rate( $from, $to );\n\t\t} catch ( ExceptionInterface $e ) {\n\t\t\t/*\n\t\t\t * Every failure the module raises implements this interface, and each carries a\n\t\t\t * message written to be read by a person: which currency is unknown, which pair\n\t\t\t * has no stored rate, what to do about it. They are sent through as-is and the\n\t\t\t * browser writes them with `textContent`, so the currency code echoed back inside\n\t\t\t * one is text on the page and never markup.\n\t\t\t *\n\t\t\t * 400 and not 500: all three are the caller asking for something impossible.\n\t\t\t */\n\t\t\twp_send_json_error( array( 'message' => $e->getMessage() ), 400 );\n\n\t\t\treturn;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t$to_code = strtoupper( $to );\n\n\t\twp_send_json_success(\n\t\t\tarray(\n\t\t\t\t'amount'    => $amount,\n\t\t\t\t'from'      => strtoupper( $from ),\n\t\t\t\t'to'        => $to_code,\n\t\t\t\t'result'    => $result,\n\t\t\t\t'formatted' => self::format( $result, $to_code ),\n\t\t\t\t// A string, like everything else that touches a stored rate.\n\t\t\t\t'rate'      => $rate,\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t);\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Render a converted amount at the target currency's minor-unit scale.\n\t *\n\t * `number_format_i18n()`, not `NumberFormatter`: `intl` is present in the WP-CLI image\n\t * and absent from the web image, so the obvious choice here is the one that passes every\n\t * `bin/wp` check and fatals on the first page load (collision C6).\n\t *\n\t * The symbol is appended rather than positioned by locale. Getting placement right for\n\t * every currency is what `intl` is for, and guessing it wrong looks worse than not\n\t * trying; the code is always shown, which is unambiguous.\n\t *\n\t * @param float  $amount The converted amount.\n\t * @param string $code   Target currency code.\n\t * @return string Something like `11,070.00 RUB`.\n\t */\n\tprivate static function format( $amount, $code ) {\n\t\t$currency = ( new WpdbCurrencyRepository() )->find( $code );\n\t\t$digits   = $currency instanceof Currency ? $currency->decimal_digits() : Currency::DEFAULT_DECIMAL_DIGITS;\n\t\t$symbol   = $currency instanceof Currency ? $currency->symbol() : '';\n\n\t\t$formatted = number_format_i18n( $amount, $digits ) . ' ' . $code;\n\n\t\treturn '' === $symbol ? $formatted : $formatted . ' (' . $symbol . ')';\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * One posted field, sanitised.\n\t *\n\t * The nonce has already been checked by the caller, which is what makes reading `$_POST`\n\t * here legitimate. Values are still sanitised and still validated downstream —\n\t * `CurrencyConverter` decides what is a currency and what is a number, and says so by\n\t * throwing.\n\t *\n\t * @param string $key Field name.\n\t * @return string Sanitised value, or an empty string.\n\t */\n\tprivate static function posted( $key ) {\n\t\t// phpcs:ignore WordPress.Security.NonceVerification.Missing -- check_ajax_referer() runs in handle() before this is reached.\n\t\tif ( ! isset( $_POST[ $key ] ) ) {\n\t\t\treturn '';\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// phpcs:ignore WordPress.Security.NonceVerification.Missing -- As above.\n\t\treturn sanitize_text_field( wp_unslash( $_POST[ $key ] ) );\n\t}\n}\n"
}

The freshness window is what bounds the module to one API\n\t * request a day (R5) and this button is exactly the case it was written for — a control a\n\t * person can click twice. It is called *after* the rates\n\t\t\t// so that a first run on a fresh install fills the table people came to look at\n\t\t\t// even if the second request fails.\n\t\t\t$currencies = $updater->update_currencies();\n\n\t\t\t$messages[] = $currencies->message();\n\n\t\t\tself::notice(\n\t\t\t\timplode( ' ', $messages ),\n\t\t\t\t$rates->is_updated() || $currencies->is_updated() ?   "content": "<?php\n/**\n * The settings screen.\n *\n * @package Currency_Converter\n */\n\nnamespace Drozd\\Currency\\Admin;\n\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Api\\ApiKey;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Api\\FreeCurrencyApiClient;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Currencies;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Db\\WpdbRateRepository;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Domain\\Rate;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Plugin;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Service\\RateUpdater;\n\ndefined( 'ABSPATH' ) || exit;\n\n/**\n * One setting, and a status panel that answers \"why is the table empty\".\n *\n * The only setting is the API key, which is the whole of what the task contract allows:\n * no rounding preferences, no display formats, no per-currency toggles. Everything else on\n * the screen is read-only — when the last sync ran, when the next one is due, how much quota\n * the last authenticated response reported, how many rates are stored.\n *\n * **The stored key never reaches the page source.** The field is `type=\"password\"` and its\n * `value` is always the empty string — not the key, not a masked stand-in of the right\n * length, nothing. Every path that does not\n\t * store something returns the value already in the database, because this callback's\n\t * return value *is* what gets written.\n\t *\n\t * @param mixed $value Whatever was submitted.\n\t * @return string The value to store.\n\t */\n\tpublic static function sanitize_api_key( $value ) {\n\t\t// Guarantees `autoload='no'` before the Settings API's own `update_option()` runs:\n\t\t// that call passes no autoload argument, and for an option that does not exist yet\n\t\t// WordPress would create it autoloaded. See `ApiKey`.\n\t\tApiKey::ensure_option_exists();\n\n\t\t$existing  = (string) get_option( ApiKey::OPTION, '' );\n\t\t$submitted = is_string( $value ) ? trim( $value ) : '';\n\n\t\tif ( '' === $submitted ) {\n\t\t\t// Empty means unchanged. The field renders empty on every load, so treating this\n\t\t\t// as a deletion would wipe the key every time the form is saved.\n\t\t\treturn $existing;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif ( ApiKey::is_from_environment() ) {\n\t\t\tadd_settings_error(\n\t\t\t\t'currency_converter',\n\t\t\t\t'currency_converter_api_key_env',\n\t\t\t\t__( 'The API key comes from the environment (FREECURRENCYAPI_KEY) and cannot be changed here. {
  "file_path": "/Users/drozd/development/test-project/web/app/plugins/currency-converter/assets/admin.css",
  "content": "/**\n * Admin styling for the currency screens.\n *\n * Small on purpose. The screens are built from core admin components — `wrap`, `card`,\n * `widefat striped`, `notice inline`, `description` — so they inherit the admin colour\n * scheme, the dark mode a site may be using, and every future change to it. What is here is\n * only what core has no class for.\n *\n * @package Currency_Converter\n */\n\n.currency-converter-rates .cc-summary {\n\tmargin: 1em 0;\n}\n\n.currency-converter-rates .cc-summary-age {\n\tcolor: #646970;\n}\n\n/* Rates and the symbols beside currency names are data: aligned and monospaced so that\n   columns of digits can be compared down the page rather than read one at a time. */\n.currency-converter-rates .cc-rate {\n\tfont-family: Consolas, Monaco, monospace;\n}\n\n.currency-converter-rates .column-rate {\n\ttext-align: right;\n}\n\n.currency-converter-rates .cc-symbol {\n\tcolor: #646970;\n}\n\n.cc-widget {\n\tmax-width: none;\n\tmargin: 1em 0;\n\tpadding: 0.5em 1.5em 1em;\n}\n\n.cc-widget-form {\n\tdisplay: flex;\n\tflex-wrap: wrap;\n\talign-items: center;\n\tgap: 0.5em;\n}\n\n.cc-widget-form label {\n\tmargin-left: 0.5em;\n\tfont-weight: 600;\n}\n\n.cc-widget-form label:first-child {\n\tmargin-left: 0;\n}\n\n/* The result takes a whole row of the flex container, so a long error message wraps under\n   the controls instead of stretching them. */\n.cc-widget-result {\n\tflex-basis: 100%;\n\tmargin: 0.5em 0 0;\n\tmin-height: 1.5em;\n\tfont-size: 1.1em;\n}\n\n.cc-widget-result.cc-ok {\n\tfont-weight: 600;\n}\n\n.cc-widget-result.cc-error {\n\tcolor: #b32d2e;\n}\n\n.cc-widget-result.cc-working {\n\tcolor: #646970;\n}\n\n.cc-status {\n\tmax-width: 50em;\n\tmargin-bottom: 1.5em;\n}\n\n.cc-status th {\n\twidth: 14em;\n}\n\n.cc-action {\n\tmargin: 0 0 1em;\n}\n\n.cc-action .description {\n\tmargin-left: 0.5em;\n}\n"
}

2	/**
3	 * Plugin lifecycle and hook registration.
4	 *
5	 * @package Currency_Converter
6	 */
7	
8	namespace Drozd\Currency;
9	
10	use Drozd\Currency\Cron\Scheduler;
11	use Drozd\Currency\Db\Schema;
12	
13	defined( 'ABSPATH' ) || exit;
14	
15	/**
16	 * Wires the module to WordPress and owns the activation and deactivation behaviour.
17	 */
18	final class Plugin {
19	
20		/**
21		 * Cron hook that refreshes the exchange rates. Daily; see R5.
22		 */
23		const CRON_HOOK_RATES = 'currency_converter_update_rates';
24	
25		/**
26		 * Cron hook that refreshes the currency metadata. Weekly; the data is near-static.
27		 */
28		const CRON_HOOK_CURRENCIES = 'currency_converter_update_currencies';
29	
30		/**
31		 * Register everything the plugin does on an ordinary request.
32		 *
33		 * Called at the bottom of the main plugin file, so hooks that fire before
34		 * `plugins_loaded` are still reachable from here.
35		 *
36		 * @return void
37		 */
38		public static function boot() {
39			// `register_activation_hook` does not fire when a plugin is updated, so the
40			// installed schema version is compared on every load and the installer re-run
41			// when it lags. This is the only way a shipped schema change reaches an
42			// existing site.
43			add_action( 'plugins_loaded', array( Schema::class, 'maybe_upgrade' ) );
44	
45			// Not deferred to `plugins_loaded` and not wrapped in any condition: the cron
46			// handlers have to be attached in a cron request, which is a front-end request, and
47			// the `init` self-heal has to be attached before `init` fires.
48			Scheduler::register();
49		}
50	
51		/**
52		 * Activation: create the tables, then schedule the syncs that fill them.
53		 *
54		 * Order matters. `Scheduler::activate()` queues a run thirty seconds out, so the tables
55		 * have to exist first — and they do, because `dbDelta()` has returned by the time the
56		 * next line runs.
57		 *
58		 * @return void
59		 */
60		public static function activate() {
61			Schema::install();
62			Scheduler::activate();
63		}
64	
65		/**
66		 * Deactivation: unschedule, and nothing else.
67		 *
68		 * Deactivation is not uninstall. Dropping tables here would destroy real data
69		 * every time someone toggles the plugin off to debug something; that belongs in
70		 * `uninstall.php`, which only runs on delete.

## User
<details>
<summary>Result</summary>

```
---
name: browser-capture
description: "Use when screenshots of the running site or its admin are needed — for a deliverable, a bug report, or a before/after comparison — or when a page has to be checked as a browser actually renders it rather than as curl returns it."
compatibility: "Playwright with headless Chromium, Node 18+. Requires the stack to be running and `npm install && npx playwright install chromium` once."
---

# Browser capture

Screenshots the site through headless Chromium. Deterministic by construction: a capture that
shifts between runs cannot be used to compare before and after.

## Usage

```bash
npm install && npx playwright install chromium     # once
make screenshots                                   # the default set

node .claude/skills/browser-capture/scripts/capture.mjs --list
node .claude/skills/browser-capture/scripts/capture.mjs \
    --shot rates=/wp/wp-admin/admin.php?page=some-page
```

Output: `docs/screenshots/NN-name.png`, numbered in capture order.

Flags: `--url`, `--user`, `--password`, `--out`, `--width`, `--height`. Defaults come from
`WP_HOME`, `WP_ADMIN_USER` and `WP_ADMIN_PASSWORD`, matching `.env`.

## Bedrock paths

The one thing a generic browser tool gets wrong here. Core is served from `/wp`:

| | |
|---|---|
| Login | `/wp/wp-login.php` |
| Admin | `/wp/wp-admin/` |
| Plugin page | `/wp/wp-admin/admin.php?page=…` |
| Front end | `/` |

A tool pointed at `/wp-login.php` gets a 404 and usually reports it as "the site is down".

## What makes the output stable

- fixed viewport (1440×900 by default) at `deviceScaleFactor: 2`
- `reducedMotion: 'reduce'`, plus injected CSS that kills every animation and transition
- the caret is made transparent — a blinking cursor otherwise changes the image between runs
- the admin-bar logo and heartbeat indicator are hidden
- `waitUntil: 'networkidle'` before each shot

Login happens once and the session is reused, so a run of ten shots does ten page loads, not ten
logins.

## Failure behaviour

One failed shot does not abort the run — the rest are still captured, the failure is printed, and
the process exits non-zero. A screenshot run that half-fails silently is how empty images end up
in a deliverable.

Two errors are explicit rather than generic, because they are the common ones:

- no login form at the expected URL → the stack is down, or the path assumption is wrong
```

</details>

Admin paths carry their own: the whole point of\n\t\t// `--shot rates=/wp/wp-admin/admin.php?page=currency-rates` is the query string, and\n\t\t// a plain split('=') drops everything from the second one onwards — the request then\n\t\t// goes to `admin.php?page` with no value, WordPress answers 403, and the screenshot\n\t\t// is a permission error that looks like a bug in the plugin being captured.\n\t\tconst separator = pair.indexOf('=');\n\n\t\tif (separator < 1) {\n\t\t\tthrow new Error(`--shot needs name=path, got \"${pair}\"`);\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn { name: pair.slice(0, separator), path: pair.slice(separator + 1) };\n\t});"

All verified. Now unit tests for the key-handling rules, since those encode the security constraints:

<details>
<summary>Tool: Write</summary>

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/drozd/development/test-project/tests/Api/ApiKeyStorageTest.php",
  "content": "<?php\n/**\n * Where the API key is read from, and what may be said about it.\n *\n * @package Tests\n */\n\ndeclare( strict_types=1 );\n\nnamespace Tests\\Api;\n\nuse Brain\\Monkey\\Functions;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Api\\ApiKey;\nuse Tests\\TestCase;\n\n/**\n * The rules that keep a credential out of the places credentials leak from.\n *\n * `FREECURRENCYAPI_KEY` is defined once per PHP process and cannot be redefined, so the\n * bootstrap defines it empty — which is exactly how `config/application.php` leaves it on a\n * checkout with no key in `.env`, and the case worth pinning here: a constant that exists but\n * holds nothing must not count as \"the environment supplies the key\", or the settings page\n * disables its own field and no key can ever be configured.\n *\n * The other direction — a non-empty constant overriding a stored option — cannot be reached\n * from this process. It is verified against the running stack instead, by starting a\n * container with the variable set; see the note in the delivery summary rather than assuming\n * this file covers it.\n */\nfinal class ApiKeyStorageTest extends TestCase {\n\n\t/**\n\t * Options as the fake `get_option`/`add_option`/`update_option` see them.\n\t *\n\t * @var array<string, mixed>\n\t */\n\tprivate array $options = array();\n\n\t/**\n\t * The `$autoload` argument each option was created with.\n\t *\n\t * @var array<string, mixed>\n\t */\n\tprivate array $autoload = array();\n\n\t/**\n\t * Options written through `update_option` rather than `add_option`.\n\t *\n\t * @var array<int, string>\n\t */\n\tprivate array $updated = array();\n\n\tprotected function set_up(): void {\n\t\tparent::set_up();\n\n\t\t$this->options  = array();\n\t\t$this->autoload = array();\n\t\t$this->updated  = array();\n\n\t\tFunctions\\when( 'get_option' )->alias(\n\t\t\tfn( string $name, $default_value = false ) => $this->options[ $name ] ?? $default_value\n\t\t);\n\n\t\tFunctions\\when( 'add_option' )->alias(\n\t\t\tfunction ( string $name, $value = '', $deprecated = '', $autoload = 'yes' ) {\n\t\t\t\tunset( $deprecated );\n\n\t\t\t\tif ( isset( $this->options[ $name ] ) ) {\n\t\t\t\t\treturn false;\n\t\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\t\t$this->options[ $name ]  = $value;\n\t\t\t\t$this->autoload[ $name ] = $autoload;\n\n\t\t\t\treturn true;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t);\n\n\t\tFunctions\\when( 'update_option' )->alias(\n\t\t\tfunction ( string $name, $value ) {\n\t\t\t\t$this->options[ $name ] = $value;\n\t\t\t\t$this->updated[]        = $name;\n\n\t\t\t\treturn true;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t);\n\n\t\tFunctions\\when( 'delete_option' )->alias(\n\t\t\tfunction ( string $name ) {\n\t\t\t\tunset( $this->options[ $name ] );\n\n\t\t\t\treturn true;\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t);\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * A constant that is defined but empty is not a configured key.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_an_empty_constant_is_not_an_environment_key(): void {\n\t\t$this->assertSame( '', constant( ApiKey::CONSTANT ), 'the bootstrap is expected to define it empty' );\n\t\t$this->assertFalse( ApiKey::is_from_environment() );\n\t\t$this->assertFalse( ApiKey::is_configured() );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * With no constant to speak of, the stored option is the key.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_the_stored_option_is_used_when_the_environment_is_empty(): void {\n\t\tApiKey::store( 'fca_live_STOREDKEY_0123456789' );\n\n\t\t$this->assertSame( 'fca_live_STOREDKEY_0123456789', ApiKey::get() );\n\t\t$this->assertTrue( ApiKey::is_configured() );\n\t\t$this->assertTrue( ApiKey::is_stored() );\n\t\t$this->assertFalse( ApiKey::is_from_environment() );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * The option is created with `autoload='no'`, which is the whole point of pre-creating it.\n\t *\n\t * An autoloaded option is read into `alloptions` on every request and cached there. The\n\t * Settings API saves through `update_option()` with no autoload argument, so if the\n\t * option does not already exist it is created with WordPress's default — autoloaded.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_the_key_option_is_never_autoloaded(): void {\n\t\tApiKey::ensure_option_exists();\n\n\t\t$this->assertArrayHasKey( ApiKey::OPTION, $this->autoload );\n\t\t$this->assertSame( 'no', $this->autoload[ ApiKey::OPTION ] );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Storing a key does not re-create the option, so its autoload setting survives.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_storing_a_key_writes_through_update_and_keeps_the_autoload_setting(): void {\n\t\tApiKey::ensure_option_exists();\n\t\tApiKey::store( 'fca_live_STOREDKEY_0123456789' );\n\n\t\t$this->assertSame( 'no', $this->autoload[ ApiKey::OPTION ] );\n\t\t$this->assertContains( ApiKey::OPTION, $this->updated );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Deleting removes it entirely.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_forget_deletes_the_option(): void {\n\t\tApiKey::store( 'fca_live_STOREDKEY_0123456789' );\n\t\tApiKey::forget();\n\n\t\t$this->assertSame( '', ApiKey::get() );\n\t\t$this->assertFalse( ApiKey::is_stored() );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * The hint reveals the last four characters and nothing else.\n\t *\n\t * Not the prefix: `fca_live_` is the same on every key the plan issues, so showing it\n\t * identifies nothing while making the visible tail look longer than it is.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_the_hint_reveals_only_the_last_four_characters(): void {\n\t\tApiKey::store( 'fca_live_SECRETVALUE_abcd' );\n\n\t\t$hint = ApiKey::hint();\n\n\t\t$this->assertStringEndsWith( 'abcd', $hint );\n\t\t$this->assertStringNotContainsString( 'SECRETVALUE', $hint );\n\t\t$this->assertStringNotContainsString( 'fca_live_', $hint );\n\t\t$this->assertSame( 4, strlen( preg_replace( '/[^A-Za-z0-9_-]/', '', $hint ) ) );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Something too short to be a key gets no hint at all.\n\t *\n\t * Four characters of a six-character string is not a hint, it is most of the value.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_a_short_key_gets_no_hint(): void {\n\t\t$this->options[ ApiKey::OPTION ] = 'abc123';\n\n\t\t$this->assertSame( '', ApiKey::hint() );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * What counts as key-shaped.\n\t *\n\t * @dataProvider provide_candidate_keys\n\t ## Assistant
A real finding: PHP's `$` anchor matches before a trailing newline, so a key ending in `\n` passed the shape check — and that value goes into an HTTP header.

<details>
<summary>Tool: Edit</summary>

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/drozd/development/test-project/web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Api/ApiKey.php",
  "old_string": "\t * @param string $key Candidate key.\n\t * @return bool True when it is worth storing.\n\t */\n\tpublic static function is_well_formed( $key ) {\n\t\tif ( ! is_string( $key ) ) {\n\t\t\treturn false;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn 1 === preg_match( '/^[A-Za-z0-9_\\-]{' . self::MIN_LENGTH . ',256}$/', $key );\n\t}",
  "new_string": "\t * The `D` modifier is not decoration. Without it `$` also matches immediately before a\n\t * final newline, so `\"[REDACTED:api-key]\\n\"` — a key pasted with the line ending\n\t * still attached, which is the single most likely way one arrives — satisfies the pattern\n\t * and is stored intact. This value is sent as an HTTP header, and a newline inside a\n\t * header value is where header injection starts.\n\t *\n\t * @param string $key Candidate key.\n\t * @return bool True when it is worth storing.\n\t */\n\tpublic static function is_well_formed( $key ) {\n\t\tif ( ! is_string( $key ) ) {\n\t\t\treturn false;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn 1 === preg_match( '/^[A-Za-z0-9_\\-]{' . self::MIN_LENGTH . ',256}$/D', $key );\n\t}"
}

## Assistant
Done. The admin layer is in [src/Admin/](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Admin/) with [ApiKey.php](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Api/ApiKey.php) alongside it, all wired through [Plugin::boot()](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Plugin.php#L38).

**The `WP_List_Table` traps** — all four are handled in [Menu.php](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Admin/Menu.php#L177-L204) and [RatesListTable.php](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Admin/RatesListTable.php):

- `class-wp-list-table.php` is required inside `load-{$hook}` only. At file level it would fatal in cron and WP-CLI, and this plugin runs in both.
- The table is constructed in that same callback, next to `add_screen_option()`. The rendered page proves it took: `name="wp_screen_options[option]" value="currency_converter_rates_per_page"` is in the source, so the Screen Options control is real and a saved per-page choice is honoured — that needed a `set_screen_option_*` filter registered at plugin load, because the POST that saves it runs before `admin_menu`.
- `_column_headers` is set in [`prepare_items()`](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Admin/RatesListTable.php#L165-L170).
- `search_box()` sits inside a `<form method="get">` with the hidden `page` field, in [RatesPage.php](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Admin/RatesPage.php#L92-L104).

**R7 — the count matches `COUNT(*)`:**

```
page header COUNT : 11      ← what the screen prints
SELECT COUNT(*)   : 11
rows listed       : 11
```

The screen shows "**11 saved rates**" above the table and "11 items" in the pager. No filter is applied by default; searching `EUR` narrows the pager to "1 item" while the summary line still reads "11 saved rates", so the total stays checkable against the database whatever the view is doing. That needed a `search` argument on `WpdbRateRepository::all()` plus a new `count_matching()` — deliberately *not* on the interface, so `count()` remains the one unfiltered number R7 is verified with.

**"Update now"** goes through `admin_post_currency_converter_update` with both `current_user_can()` and `check_admin_referer()` ([UpdateAction.php](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Admin/UpdateAction.php#L152-L166)). It does not force the sync — the freshness window is what bounds the module to one request a day, and this button is the double-click case it was written for.

**The key never reaches the page source.** With a key stored, the settings page renders:

```html
<input type="password" ... value="" ... placeholder="Stored — leave blank to keep it">
```

`grep -c TESTKEY` over the full 56 KB of page source returns **0**; what is shown instead is `A key is stored (••••••••1234)`. Empty submission means unchanged, deletion is a separate nonced button, and the option is created with `autoload='no'` (verified: `SELECT autoload` returns `off`) so the key never enters the `alloptions` cache.

**The AJAX widget**, live over `admin-ajax.php`:

```
123 USD → RUB : {"result":11439.8761851741,"formatted":"11,439.88 RUB","rate":"93.007123456700"}
123 EUR → JPY : {"result":20025.688688717062,"formatted":"20,026 JPY (¥)"}   ← 0 decimals, JPY's minor unit
123 USD → TRY : "TRY is a known currency, but no USD to TRY rate is stored…"
bad nonce     : -1
logged out    : 0
```

Two things I fixed along the way, both worth knowing about:

The capture script split `--shot name=path` on *every* `=`, so `admin.php?page=currency-rates` reached the browser as `admin.php?page` and WordPress answered 403. The first screenshots looked like a permission bug in the plugin. Fixed in [capture.mjs](.claude/skills/browser-capture/scripts/capture.mjs#L49-L61) — its own usage example had the same shape and would have failed identically.

A test caught that `is_well_formed()` accepted a key with a trailing newline: PHP's `$` anchor matches before a final `\n` without the `D` modifier. A pasted key with its line ending attached is the most likely way one arrives, and that value goes straight into an HTTP header. Fixed with `/D`.

`make lint` and `make test` are green (185 tests), the `everapihq` and `intl` gates both return nothing, and screenshots are in [docs/screenshots/](docs/screenshots/).

One flag on the plan file you have open: it lists a `[currency_convert]` shortcode, which `.claude/agents/_TASK_CONTRACT.md` names explicitly as a non-goal ("No shortcode, block, widget, or front-end display"). Worth reconciling before that phase starts rather than during review.

## Why error-429.json is not captured live

quota. Neither is an acceptable price for a fixture, so the body is written from the
documented shape. It is a single field — `{"message":"Too Many Requests"}` — and the

does not say whether the minute or the month was exhausted, and the tests supply those

That is a convenience, and the risk with any such convenience is\n * that its output becomes indistinguishable from the real thing an hour later — a screenshot\n * in a review, a number quoted in a meeting, a developer who forgets which site they are on.\n *\n * So the fixture loader is not allowed to be quiet. It sets this flag, and while the flag is\n * set every surface that displays a rate says where it came from: the admin table carries a\n * banner, `wp currency rates status` prints it, and `wp currency doctor` reports it as a\n * warning rather than a pass.\n *\n * **What deliberately does not happen** is as important. Loading a fixture does not write\n * `RateUpdater::LAST_SYNC_OPTION`, does not touch the quota reading, and does not stamp the\n * rows with the current time — they carry the fixture's own `captured_at`, weeks in the past.\n * Nothing about the resulting state imitates a completed fetch: the rates read as old because\n * they are old, and `RateUpdater` sees them as stale, so a real sync is never delayed by a\n * demo having been loaded.\n *\n * The flag is cleared by the only thing that earns clearing it — a successful sync against the\n * live API, which overwrites every row it describes.\n */\nfinal class DemoMode {\n\n\t/**\n\t * Option holding the demo-mode record. Not autoloaded; it is read on our screens only.\n\t */\n\tconst OPTION = 'currency_converter_demo';\n\n\t/**\n\t * Record that fixture data has been loaded.\n\t *\n\t * @param string             $source      Where the fixture came from, for display.\n\t * @param \\DateTimeImmutable $captured_at The date the fixture claims for its data.\n\t * @return void\n\t */\n\tpublic static function enable( $source, \\DateTimeImmutable $captured_at ) {\n\t\tupdate_option(\n\t\t\tself::OPTION,\n\t\t\tarray(\n\t\t\t\t'source'      => (string) $source,\n\t\t\t\t'captured_at' => $captured_at->format( 'c' ),\n\t\t\t\t'loaded_at'   => gmdate( 'c' ),\n\t\t\t),\n\t\t\t'no'\n\t\t);\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Clear the flag.\n\t *\n\t * Called after a successful live sync, which has just overwritten the fixture rows.\n\t *\n\t * @return void\n\t */\n\tpublic static function clear() {\n\t\tdelete_option( self::OPTION );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Whether the stored rates are demonstration data.\n\t *\n\t * @return bool True while a fixture is loaded and no live sync has replaced it.\n\t */\n\tpublic static function is_active() {\n\t\treturn null !== self::details();\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * What was loaded, and when.\n\t *\n\t * @return array{source: string, captured_at: string, loaded_at: string}|null The record,\n\t *                                                                            or null.\n\t */\n\tpublic static function details() {\n\t\t$stored = get_option( self::OPTION );\n\n\t\tif ( ! is_array( $stored ) || ! isset( $stored['source'] ) ) {\n\t\t\treturn null;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn array(\n\t\t\t'source'      => (string) $stored['source'],\n\t\t\t'captured_at' => isset( $stored['captured_at'] ) ? (string) $stored['captured_at'] : '',\n\t\t\t'loaded_at'   => isset( $stored['loaded_at'] ) ? (string) $stored['loaded_at'] : '',\n\t\t);\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * The one sentence every surface repeats.\n\t *\n\t * Kept here rather than written out at each call site so the admin banner, the CLI and\n\t * the doctor cannot drift into saying three different things about the same state.\n\t *\n\t * @return string The warning.\n\t */\n\tpublic static function warning() {\n\t\treturn __( 'Demo data, not live rates.', 'currency-converter' );\n\t}\n}\n"
