  "content": "<?php\n/**\n * `wp currency rates`.\n *\n * @package Currency_Converter\n */\n\nnamespace Drozd\\Currency\\Cli;\n\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Api\\ApiKey;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Currencies;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Db\\WpdbRateRepository;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\DemoMode;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Domain\\Rate;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Plugin;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Service\\FixtureLoader;\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Service\\RateUpdater;\nuse WP_CLI;\nuse WP_CLI\\Utils;\n\ndefined( 'ABSPATH' ) || exit;\n\n/**\n * Fetch, list and inspect the stored exchange rates.\n *\n * Every subcommand here reports what happened rather than what it hoped for. A sync that was\n * skipped says it was skipped and why; a sync that failed exits non-zero, because an exit\n * code is the only part of this a script can act on.\n */\nfinal class RatesCommand {\n\n\t/**\n\t * Fetch the latest rates and store them.\n\t *\n\t * Ordinarily bounded to one fetch a day: the freshness window in `RateUpdater` refuses a\n\t * second one and says so, spending no quota. That bound is the whole reason a monthly\n\t * allowance of 5,000 requests is never in danger, so `--force` is the deliberate way past\n\t * it and not the default.\n\t *\n\t * ## OPTIONS\n\t *\n\t * [--force]\n\t * : Ignore the 23-hour freshness window and fetch anyway. Spends one API request. The\n\t * lock still applies, so two forced runs at once still make one request.\n\t *\n\t * ## EXAMPLES\n\t *\n\t *     wp currency rates update\n\t *     wp currency rates update --force\n\t *\n\t * @param array<int, string>    $args       Positional arguments; none.\n\t * @param array<string, string> $assoc_args Associative arguments.\n\t * @return void\n\t */\n\tpublic function update( $args, $assoc_args ) {\n\t\tunset( $args );\n\n\t\tif ( ! ApiKey::is_configured() ) {\n\t\t\tWP_CLI::error( 'No API key is configured. Set FREECURRENCYAPI_KEY in .env, or save one on the settings screen.' );\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t$force = (bool) Utils\\get_flag_value( $assoc_args, 'force', false );\n\n\t\ttry {\n\t\t\t$result = RateUpdater::from_config()->update_rates( $force );\n\t\t} catch ( \\Throwable $e ) {\n\t\t\t// Non-zero exit. A sync that could not reach the API is a failure, and a caller\n\t\t\t// that cannot tell it from a skip will happily build on rates that never arrived.\n\t\t\tWP_CLI::error( $e->getMessage() );\n\n\t\t\treturn;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif ( $result->is_updated() ) {\n\t\t\tWP_CLI::success( $result->message() );\n\n\t\t\treturn;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Not a failure and not a success: nothing was fetched, on purpose. `log` rather than\n\t\t// `warning`, because being inside the freshness window is the system working.\n\t\tWP_CLI::log( $result->message() );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * List the stored rates.\n\t *\n\t * ## OPTIONS\n\t *\n\t * [--base=<code>]\n\t * : Only rates quoted against this base currency.\n\t *\n\t * [--search=<term>]\n\t * : Only rates whose base or target code contains this.\n\t *\n\t * [--orderby=<column>]\n\t * : Sort column.\n\t * ---\n\t * default: target_code\n\t * options:\n\t *   - base_code\n\t *   - target_code\n\t *   - rate\n\t *   - fetched_at\n\t * ---\n\t *\n\t * [--order=<direction>]\n\t * : Sort direction.\n\t * ---\n\t * default: asc\n\t * options:\n\t *   - asc\n\t *   - desc\n\t * ---\n\t *\n\t * [--fields=<fields>]\n\t * : Columns to show.\n\t * ---\n\t * default: base_code,target_code,name,rate,fetched_at\n\t * ---\n\t *\n\t * [--format=<format>]\n\t * : Output format.\n\t * ---\n\t * default: table\n\t * options:\n\t *   - table\n\t *   - csv\n\t *   - json\n\t *   - yaml\n\t *   - count\n\t * ---\n\t *\n\t * ## EXAMPLES\n\t *\n\t *     wp currency rates list\n\t *     wp currency rates list --format=count\n\t *     wp currency rates list --search=EUR --format=csv\n\t *\n\t * @param array<int, string>    $args       Positional arguments; 'loaded in this runtime (' . PHP_SAPI . '); check the web container separately'\n\t\t\t\t: 'missing — rates are DECIMAL(24,12) and float arithmetic cannot hold them'\n\t\t);\n\n\t\t$checks[] = self::result(\n\t\t\t'php.intl',\n\t\t\textension_loaded( 'intl' ) ? self::WARN : self::OK,\n\t\t\textension_loaded( 'intl' )\n\t\t\t\t// Backwards on purpose: the hazard is *depending* on intl, because it is in\n\t\t\t\t// the CLI image and not the web one. Its presence here is the trap, not a pass.\n\t\t\t\t? 'present here but absent from the web image — nothing in the module may use NumberFormatter'\n\t\t\t\t: 'absent, as expected; formatting uses number_format_i18n()'\n\t\t);\n\n\t\treturn $checks;\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Tables and schema version.\n\t *\n\t * @return array<int, array{check: string, status: string, detail: string}> Check results.\n\t */\n\tprivate function schema_checks() {\n\t\tglobal $wpdb;\n\n\t\t$checks = array();\n\n\t\tforeach ( array( 'rates' => Schema::rates_table(), 'currencies' => Schema::currencies_table() ) as $label => $table ) {\n\t\t\t// phpcs:ignore WordPress.DB.DirectDatabaseQuery.DirectQuery, WordPress.DB.DirectDatabaseQuery.NoCaching -- A schema probe; there is nothing to cache and no cache to invalidate.\n\t\t\t$found = $wpdb->get_var( $wpdb->prepare( 'SHOW TABLES LIKE %s', $table ) );\n\n\t\t\t$checks[] = self::result(\n\t\t\t\t'db.table.' . $label,\n\t\t\t\t$found === $table ? self::OK : self::FAIL,\n\t\t\t\t$found === $table ? $table : $table . 'none' : $installed, Schema::VERSION )\n\t\t);\n\n\t\treturn $checks;\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * The API key and what the last authenticated response said about the quota.\n\t *\n\t * @return array<int, array{check: string, status: string, detail: string}> Check results.\n\t */\n\tprivate function credential_checks() {\n\t\t$checks = array();\n\n\t\t$configured = ApiKey::is_configured();\n\n\t\t$checks[] = self::result(\n\t\t\t'api.key',\n\t\t\t$configured ? self::OK : self::FAIL,\n\t\t\t$configured\n\t\t\t\t// The hint and never the key: this output gets pasted into issues.\n\t\t\t\t? sprintf( '%s, from the %s', ApiKey::hint(), ApiKey::is_from_environment() ? 'no rates stored — run `wp currency rates update`'\n\t\t\t\t: sprintf( '%d stored, %d currencies served', $stored, $expected )\n\t\t);\n\n\t\tif ( null === $fetched_at ) {\n\t\t\t$checks[] = self::result( 'data.age', self::FAIL, 'never fetched' );\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\t$age = time() - $fetched_at->getTimestamp();\n\n\t\t\t$checks[] = self::result(\n\t\t\t\t'data.age',\n\t\t\t\t$age > 2 * DAY_IN_SECONDS ? self::WARN : self::OK,\n\t\t\t\tsprintf( '%s old', human_time_diff( $fetched_at->getTimestamp() ) )\n\t\t\t);\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t$demo = DemoMode::details();\n\n\t\t$checks[] = self::result(\n\t\t\t'data.source',\n\t\t\tnull === $demo ? self::OK : self::WARN,\n\t\t\tnull === $demo\n\t\t\t\t? The class files themselves are only ever loaded by\n * the autoloader when one of them is named, which happens inside this guard, so a web request\n * never even reads them.\n *\n * The `currency` namespace is created implicitly by registering commands beneath it; there is\n * no parent class to write, and inventing one would only add a `wp currency` that does\n * nothing.\n */\nfinal class Commands {\n\n\t/**\n\t * Register the commands.\n\t *\n\t * @return void\n\t */\n\tpublic static function register() {\n\t\tif ( ! defined( 'WP_CLI' ) || ! WP_CLI ) {\n\t\t\treturn;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tWP_CLI::add_command( 'currency rates', RatesCommand::class );\n\t\tWP_CLI::add_command( 'currency currencies', CurrenciesCommand::class );\n\t\tWP_CLI::add_command( 'currency convert', ConvertCommand::class );\n\t\tWP_CLI::add_command( 'currency doctor', DoctorCommand::class );\n\t}\n}\n"
}

  "content": "<?php\n/**\n * `[currency_convert]`.\n *\n * @package Currency_Converter\n */\n\nnamespace Drozd\\Currency;\n\nuse Drozd\\Currency\\Exception\\ExceptionInterface;\n\ndefined( 'ABSPATH' ) || exit;\n\n/**\n * `[currency_convert amount=\"123\" from=\"USD\" to=\"RUB\"]` — the service, outside wp-admin.\n *\n * Its purpose is demonstration: the same `convert()` the admin widget and the CLI call,\n * reached from a post. So it is deliberately twenty lines of work and no more — no formatting\n * options, no currency picker, no caching, no template. Anything larger would be a front-end\n * feature, and the brief asks for a service and an admin page.\n *\n * **A failure renders nothing.** An unknown currency or a missing rate throws, and on a public\n * page the exception message is the wrong answer twice over: it puts internal detail\n * (\"run `wp currency rates update --force`\") in front of a reader who cannot act on it, and it\n * lets a shortcode attribute steer text onto the page. Visitors get an empty string; an\n * administrator looking at the same page gets the message, because they are the person who can\n * fix it.\n *\n * Escaped on output like anything else. `shortcode_atts()` sanitises nothing on its own — it\n * fills in defaults — so every attribute is treated as what it is: text from a post, which on\n * a multi-author site is text from someone who is not an administrator.\n */\nfinal class Shortcode {\n\n\t/**\n\t * The shortcode tag.\n\t */\n\tconst TAG = 'currency_convert';\n\n\t/**\n\t * Register the shortcode.\n\t *\n\t * On `init`, which is where shortcodes belong: earlier and the textdomain is not loaded,\n\t * later and `the_content` may already have run.\n\t *\n\t * @return void\n\t */\n\tpublic static function register() {\n\t\tadd_action(\n\t\t\t'init',\n\t\t\tstatic function () {\n\t\t\t\tadd_shortcode( self::TAG, array( self::class, 'render' ) );\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t);\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Render one conversion.\n\t *\n\t * @param array<string, string>|string $atts Shortcode attributes.\n\t * @return string The converted amount, escaped, or an empty string on failure.\n\t */\n\tpublic static function render( $atts ) {\n\t\t$atts = shortcode_atts(\n\t\t\tarray(\n\t\t\t\t'amount' => '1',\n\t\t\t\t'from'   => Currencies::BASE,\n\t\t\t\t'to'     => 'EUR',\n\t\t\t),\n\t\t\tis_array( $atts ) ? $atts : array(),\n\t\t\tself::TAG\n\t\t);\n\n\t\ttry {\n\t\t\t$result = currency_converter()->convert(\n\t\t\t\tsanitize_text_field( $atts['amount'] ),\n\t\t\t\tsanitize_text_field( $atts['from'] ),\n\t\t\t\tsanitize_text_field( $atts['to'] )\n\t\t\t);\n\t\t} catch ( ExceptionInterface $e ) {\n\t\t\t// The reader gets nothing; whoever can fix it gets the reason.\n\t\t\treturn current_user_can( 'manage_options' )\n\t\t\t\t? '<span class=\"currency-convert-error\">' . esc_html( $e->getMessage() ) . '</span>'\n\t\t\t\t: '';\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn '<span class=\"currency-convert\">' . esc_html( number_format_i18n( $result, 2 ) ) . '</span>';\n\t}\n}\n"
}

$this->write_fixture(\n\t\t\tarray(\n\t\t\t\t'data' => array(\n\t\t\t\t\t'EUR' => 0.9145678901,\n\t\t\t\t\t'RUB' => 93.0071234567,\n\t\t\t\t),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t);\n\n\t\t$loaded = $this->loader()->load( $path );\n\n\t\t// Two rates plus the identity row the repository always writes.\n\t\t$this->assertSame( 3, $loaded['rates'] );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * A file with nothing usable in it is an error, not an empty success.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_a_fixture_without_rates_is_rejected(): void {\n\t\t$path = $this->write_fixture( array( 'captured_at' => '2026-08-01 12:00:00' ) );\n\n\t\t$this->expectException( \\RuntimeException::class );\n\t\t$this->expectExceptionMessage( 'No rates found' );\n\n\t\t$this->loader()->load( $path );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * A missing file is an error too.\n\t */\n\tpublic function test_a_missing_file_is_rejected(): void {\n\t\t$this->expectException( \\RuntimeException::class );\n\t\t$this->expectExceptionMessage( 'Cannot read' );\n\n\t\t$this->loader()->load( '/nonexistent/fixture.json' );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * The loader under test.\n\t *\n\t * @return FixtureLoader Configured loader.\n\t */\n\tprivate function loader(): FixtureLoader {\n\t\treturn new FixtureLoader( $this->rates, $this->currencies );\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Write a fixture to a temporary file.\n\t *\n\t * @param array<string, mixed> $payload What to write.\n\t * @return string Absolute path to the file.\n\t */\n\tprivate function write_fixture( array $payload ): string {\n\t\t$path = tempnam( sys_get_temp_dir(), 'cc-fixture-' ) . '.json';\n\n\t\tfile_put_contents( $path, (string) json_encode( $payload ) );\n\n\t\t$this->written[] = $path;\n\n\t\treturn $path;\n\t}\n}\n"

## Assistant
Done. Everything is built and exercised against the running stack.

**The CLI** — [src/Cli/](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Cli/), registered behind a `defined('WP_CLI')` guard because `WP_CLI::add_command()` does not exist in a web request:

```
wp currency convert <amount> <from> <to> [--format=<format>]
wp currency currencies <command>          sync, list
wp currency doctor [--format=<format>]
wp currency rates <command>               update, list, status, load-fixture
```

`convert` calls the same `CurrencyConverter` the admin widget does — no arithmetic is reimplemented, so the two cannot disagree. `123 USD → RUB` gives `11439.876185174` with `rate 1 USD = 93.007123456700 RUB`; an unknown currency exits **1** with the supported list. `--format=value` prints the bare number for `$(...)` capture.

`doctor` runs 14 checks and exits non-zero on any failure. Two rows are deliberately contrarian: `php.bcmath` names the runtime it looked at (`loaded in this runtime (cli); check the web container separately`), and `php.intl` reports **WARN when intl is present**, because its presence in the CLI image and absence from the web image is the trap, not a pass.

**`rates load-fixture`** is guarded by `WP_DEBUG` — with `WP_ENV=production` it refuses outright. The design point that took the most care is the date. [FixtureLoader](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Service/FixtureLoader.php#L228-L250) clamps every row's `fetched_at` to at least 25 hours ago, whatever the file claims:

> a fixture dated "now" would tell `RateUpdater` the rates are fresh and suppress the first real sync for a day — a demonstration aid becoming the reason the live data never arrived.

So the rows read as three weeks old because they are, and nothing writes a "last sync" timestamp or touches the quota. There is no shared code path with `RateUpdater` at all — no client, no lock, no freshness window, because none of those describe reading a file. Loading prints:

```
Loaded 33 rates and 33 currencies from …/fixtures/demo.json.
Rows are dated 2026-08-01 12:00:00 UTC, which is what the fixture states — they are not,
and do not claim to be, current.
Warning: Demo data, not live rates. Run `wp currency rates update` for live data.
```

`DemoMode` then puts the same sentence on every surface — the rates screen carries a warning banner naming the file and the date, `rates status` reports `data_source: FIXTURE`, `doctor` downgrades `data.source` to WARN, and `convert` warns on every call including `--format=value` (the warning goes to stderr, so a captured value stays clean). A successful live sync clears it, and a *skipped* sync does not — both are tested, because a fresh-skip that cleared the flag would silently relabel fixture rows as live.

**The shortcode** is 20 lines of work in [Shortcode.php](web/app/plugins/currency-converter/src/Shortcode.php). Verified in a real front-end request, not just WP-CLI: a published post rendered `<span class="currency-convert">11,439.88</span>` (post created, fetched, deleted). Failures render nothing to visitors and the message to administrators — an exception here would otherwise put `run \`wp currency rates update --force\`` in front of a reader who cannot act on it.

Two WP-CLI behaviours cost a round trip each and are now documented in the code:

- A method named `load_fixture` registers as `load_fixture`, not `load-fixture`. It needs an explicit `@subcommand load-fixture` tag.
- A `---\ndefault: the plugin's own fixtures/demo.json\n---` block in an option's docblock is injected as the literal *value*, so the described default became a filename nobody could open. Defaults that aren't literals belong in prose.

**Counts still reconcile**, which is the C2 acceptance:

```
wp currency rates list --format=count : 33
SELECT COUNT(*)                       : 33
```

`make lint` and `make test` are green (195 tests), and both delivery gates are clean.

Two documents corrected rather than left disagreeing with the code. `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` C2 said `wp currency list --format=count`, which is not the command shape you specified — updated to `wp currency rates list`. And I amended the shortcode non-goal in `.claude/agents/_TASK_CONTRACT.md` rather than quietly violating it: it now records that `[currency_convert]` was asked for explicitly after the non-goal was cited, fixes its scope at what you described, and keeps the block/widget/display exclusions standing.

One thing to know about the current state: the stack is now **in demo mode with 33 fixture rates**, replacing the 11 partial rows that were there before. There is no API key configured, so that is the most useful state to leave it in — and it is labelled as demo data on every surface. `wp currency rates update` replaces it the moment a key exists.
